The section states the requirement a framework must meet to earn evidential standing, that it forbid at least one observable pattern, and assesses that the framework as stated across eleven articles forbids very little. It then separates the three claims that survive. The independence finding survives because it was a falsifiable structural implication of the product notation, was tested against three cases, and failed in the direction the evidence indicated rather than the direction the framework's authors would have preferred, so the falsification arrived from within the exercise rather than from an external critic. The capital-formation mechanics survive because their objects are instruments rather than outcomes and are checkable against partnership agreements, securities filings, and award records without reference to any venture's success. The negative result on ownership form survives because it compares institutional forms against a stated criterion and does not depend on any venture's outcome. The section concedes that the central conjunctive thesis does not survive in predictive form, states the prospective scoring test that would settle it, namely scoring a set of ventures on all ten conditions before outcomes are known and recording the scores publicly, and notes that neither this framework nor any alternative in the literature has been subjected to such a test. | 2026-08-04 | A292: Equation-density expansion pass complete. 57 display equations added, 14 to 71, comparable to A290 at 77 and A291 at 78, with the per-section distribution matched against those two at-parity siblings and the six sections carrying zero equations in every sibling preserved at zero. The closer's equations differ in kind from the rest of the series because they formalize claims about the framework rather than about the case. The factor representation, writing each observable condition as a function of a lower-dimensional vector of underlying properties, is the central device and carries the argument across four sections. The covariance decomposition under that factor structure establishes formally why conditions sharing a loading cannot be independent, which is the technical content of the independence-failure claim. The paired inequalities showing joint attainment above the product of marginals and joint survival below it are stated adjacently so their compatibility is visible, since a reader encountering them separately would take them for a contradiction. The shared-shock indicator model supplies the mechanism producing correlated failure and identifies the loadings as the quantities that determine how far joint survival falls below the independent product. The mutual-independence requirement is stated explicitly to show that the product form demands a considerably stronger condition than pairwise independence and that nothing in the series' construction ensures it. The reproduction-probability contrast between the tenth power under independence and the fourth power under the factor reading is the article's most consequential practical implication and is now stated as an equation rather than as prose. The succession-partition array shows that each available resolution surrenders a different condition and that no row preserves all ten, and the single-component implication that a governance failure zeroes the whole conjunction explains why the succession question matters to the framework rather than only to the firm. The failure-mode ranking criterion, combining event probability with the fraction of the configuration the event removes, makes the ordering auditable rather than asserted. The leg-sequence ordering establishes that the three capital-formation legs form a dependency chain rather than a chronology, since each financed the capability the next required. Metrics after the pass: 844 lines, ~11,249 words, 71 display equations, 27 H2 sections, 10 H3 subsections, 101 total reference anchors unchanged with zero missing, unused, or duplicate anchors and zero duplicate URLs. | 2026-08-04 | A292: History of SpaceX Synthesis and Projection article drafted at `_drafts/spacex_history_synthesis_and_projection.markdown` for editorial date 2026-08-04 at 09:00 UTC. Closing article of the twelve-article series. The article has three tasks. The retrospective task restates all ten conditions with the finding each component article reached. The critical task is the principal contribution and argues that the framework's independence assumption fails, generalizing from three instances the series encountered independently in articles written for unrelated purposes: the A288 finding that a portfolio which appears to distribute risk concentrates it on a shared vehicle family, the A290 finding that sub-properties which appear to fail independently fail together under adverse states, and the A291 finding that a leg which appears to remove a constraint substitutes a different one. The correction is that the conditions are approximately separable in favorable states and correlated in adverse ones. The article then shows this biases the assessment in opposite directions and that both directions matter. Positive dependence in favorable states means the joint probability exceeds the product of the marginals, so the singular-conjunction thesis overstates rarity, and the correct reading is that a smaller number of underlying properties, plausibly a mission-directed objective function, a control configuration permitting its pursuit, a technically general capability, and access to a government customer, generates most of the ten conditions as consequences. Correlation in adverse states means joint survival falls below the product, so satisfying nine of ten conditions is not ninety percent of the way to the configuration because the conditions that fail are the ones that fail together. The article takes up both cross-condition interactions A288 recorded, establishing that the key-person dependency is perfectly correlated across every line so the portfolio supplies zero mitigation against it, and that conditions six and seven interact adversely because the concentration of decision authority that protects the mission from capital capture is precisely what makes a key-person event maximally consequential. The succession question A287 identified is accordingly ranked first among failure modes rather than treated as one weakness among ten. The projective task states its method before its conclusions, enumerates assumptions per projection, identifies the assumption each projection is most sensitive to, and ranks failure modes rather than listing them, reaching the structural conclusion that the configuration is not a steady state but a transitional arrangement whose defining feature is the continued involvement of a specific individual, so every projection to 2050 is a projection about what replaces it. Also assesses the Anduril, OpenAI, Palantir, and Blue Origin templates with the finding that each fails the conjunction at a different point, which is the pattern a genuinely conjunctive requirement produces. States the framework's successes with the same directness as its failures, including its diagnostic performance in locating a specific structural failure in each negation case. Metrics: 598 lines, ~8,348 words, 14 display equations, 27 H2 sections, 10 H3 subsections, 101 total reference anchors (44 books, 26 primary references, 11 research, 20 related-post) with zero missing, unused, or duplicate anchors and zero duplicate URLs. Both density figures are lower at initial draft than any sibling because a synthesis article introduces few new sources. Every anchor was drawn from the A290 and A291 apparatus, the two fully verified and repaired articles in the series, and the nineteen anchors identified as fabricated or unregistered in the citation-integrity audit were excluded from the source pool. All 26 primary URLs rechecked with zero 404 responses. Style discipline clean and all LaTeX macros within the MathJax default package set. Both density passes outstanding. A292 back-references A288 through A291 by post_url and all four are unpublished, so the publication batch is now five articles. No further article in this series is planned. Next available article number A295. |

The cross-side externality test shows consumer broadband fails the two-sided-market condition because additional subscribers degrade shared-cell service, so importing platform conclusions into the broadband business is an error the commentary makes routinely. The latency ratio shows the low-orbit configuration moves the service across an application threshold rather than improving along an existing performance dimension, which is the reading that explains the geostationary incumbents' non-response. The bring-into-use condition is restated as a deployment-rate requirement and the filing option shown to collapse to zero on failure rather than degrading gracefully, which is the formal core of the spectrum-priority finding. The orbital-congestion externality is given the quadratic conjunction scaling and the private-against-social optimum divergence. The steady-state fleet-age result establishes that the replenishment treadmill keeps the deployed constellation permanently averaging half a design lifetime, which is a favorable property the article had not previously stated. | 2026-08-03 | A291: History of SpaceX Category-Dominating Commercial Spinoff article drafted at `_drafts/spacex_history_category_dominating_spinoff.markdown` for editorial date 2026-08-03 at 09:00 UTC. Eleventh article of the series and third and last of the capital-formation legs. Organizing claim is that the spinoff is the internalization of an anchor customer rather than a diversification into an adjacent market, so that where the A283 Anchor Demand article treats a government customer buying launches this article treats the venture becoming the customer it had previously needed someone else to be. The decisive economic property is that the spinoff consumes the parent's output at marginal cost while every competitor must pay a market price the parent sets, giving a per-launch disadvantage equal to the parent's entire margin and an aggregate disadvantage scaling linearly in campaign size, which is why the advantage matters for a constellation and would not matter for a business requiring a single launch. Walks the January 2015 Seattle announcement and the surplus-capacity argument the article treats as the stronger rationale, the deployment sequence from the May 2019 first operational batch through the 2020 beta and 2021 rollout, the vertical integration and internal transfer price, the launch-cadence coupling in which the constellation raises cumulative flight count and thereby lowers its own launch cost, the subscriber and revenue trajectory with the segment mix shifting toward higher-value lines, the direct-to-cell extension, the replenishment treadmill, the regulatory position across the Commission the Union and national regulators, spectrum priority, and the orbital congestion externality. Three findings are new to the series. The deployment threshold means a partially deployed constellation earns approximately nothing rather than a proportional fraction, which is the structural reason the business must be financed entirely ahead of revenue and which connects this leg to the two preceding ones. Spectrum priority under the first-filed and bring-into-use regime is the point at which the capital-formation account and the regulatory account become the same account, because captive launch capacity is the mechanism by which a regulatory option is exercised before it expires and a competitor without it faces a deadline it cannot control. The replenishment treadmill converts the constellation from a capital asset into a consumable, so incumbency advantage scales with the ratio of satellite lifetime to competitor deployment time and is therefore weak, which limits the durability of the position in a way both advocates and critics underweight. The internal transfer price is identified as redistributing profit between segments while leaving the consolidated total unchanged, converging with the A288 Portfolio Patience finding from the opposite direction on the same missing number. The first is the implied post-money valuation for the January 2015 round, where the reported one billion dollars for approximately ten percent implies ten billion post-money and nine billion pre-money against a reported figure of approximately twelve billion; the discrepancy is stated rather than reconciled, because the record does not establish whether the reported figure is pre-money or post-money, whether the round carried a secondary component that would not dilute, or whether the percentage is approximate to within the required margin. The third is an explicit closure matrix in the Comparative Cross-Sectional Analysis section, marking cells the configuration renders inapplicable separately from cells the available record does not establish, with neither imputed; the two failed cases carry the most missing cells, which is precisely the pattern a survivorship problem produces and which the section now states. | 2026-08-02 | A290: History of SpaceX Patient-Private Capital-Formation Leg article drafted at `_drafts/spacex_history_patient_private_leg.markdown` for editorial date 2026-08-02 at 09:00 UTC. Tenth article of the series and second of the three capital-formation legs. Organizing claim is that patience is a structural property instruments manufacture rather than a temperament investors possess. The fund-life clock is identified as the binding constraint, operating through the contractual term, the realization-triggered carried interest, and the distributed-capital metric governing the successor fundraise, and the required holding period is shown to exceed the nominal fund life by roughly a factor of two. The semi-annual tender-offer mechanism is treated as the decisive structural innovation because it renders realization independent of company exit and thereby satisfies all three channels. Walks the 2008 Founders Fund entry, the 2009 Draper Fisher Jurvetson entry, the January 2015 Google and Fidelity round, the round and valuation sequence, investor-base composition and horizon heterogeneity, and dilution management. Treats the interaction with the government-anchor leg in both directions and shows the two legs interact multiplicatively on the terminal founder share. Negation cases are Iridium, whose debt claim converted a delay into a default, and OneWeb, where a nominally patient investor withdrew and the absence of a secondary market made a single withdrawal terminal. Treats the contemporary defense-technology venture wave with the finding that the secondary-market condition is available to few ventures. Metrics: 770 lines, ~11,668 words, 30 display equations, 27 H2 sections, 10 H3 subsections, 157 total reference anchors (54 books, 53 primary references, 31 research, 19 related-post) with zero missing, unused, or duplicate anchors and zero duplicate URLs. Ninth article of the twelve-article series and the first of the three capital-formation legs. Article's organizing distinction is between anchor demand, which A283 treats and which concerns a customer buying an output, and government-anchor capital formation, which concerns the mechanism by which the government supplied capital to build a capability before any output existed and supplied it without taking equity or votes. Treats SBIR Phase III sole-source authority as the closest statutory analogue and concludes the leg transfers position rather than money. Records the mechanism-design result that optimal incentive power falls with uncertainty as being in direct tension with the policy lesson usually drawn from the COTS case. Article's central move is distinguishing variance reduction, which the corporate-diversification literature evaluates and finds value-destroying, from ruin-probability reduction, which is the objective a mission-directed venture holds, and showing the two recommend different portfolios. | 2026-06-26 | A173-A187 series: Fifteen-article two-dimensional projection in games series researched, written, and published as a single back-dated batch across 2026-04-18 through 2026-05-02. Six clusters: A173 opener (Two-Dimensional Projection as a Coordinate Mapping Problem), A174-A178 Cartesian cluster (top-down without height, decoupled vertical axis, side-scrolling without depth, side-scrolling with parallax layers, belt-scroll with explicit depth), A179-A180 oblique-and-axonometric cluster (oblique and quarter view, axonometric with isometric/dimetric/trimetric), A181-A184 affine-and-projective cluster (Mode 7 affine ground plane, sprite scaling pseudo-three-dimensional, raycasting, stylised hybrid projections), A185-A186 cross-cutters (draw order with Y-sort/Z-sort/painter's algorithm, picking and hit testing), A187 synthesis closer (the camera as linear operator). Each article carries the standard projection-mode template (Brief History, Forward Map, Inverse Map, Worked Example, Variations, Delivery Mechanisms, Where the Framing Breaks Down, Canon, Out of Scope, Conclusion, References), with appropriate variations for the opener, cross-cutters, and synthesis closer. The y-down depth-into-screen convention is established in A174 and carried throughout the series. Forward references in prose to be converted to {% post_url %} Liquid tags in a follow-up pass. Two-commit publication pattern: draft commit captures all fifteen drafts in _drafts/; publication commit batch-moves all fifteen to _posts/ with date prefix. Articles back-dated to 2026-04-18 through 2026-05-02, landing flush with the patent and startup strategy series at 2026-05-03. Process files synced (TASKLOG.md, draft_summary.md, REVERSE_PROMPT.md). |

| 2026-06-29 | A188-A199 series: Twelve-article stream-based compilers series researched, written, and published as a single back-dated batch across 2026-04-06 through 2026-04-17. Five clusters: A188 opener (Compilation as a Streaming Discipline), A189-A191 historical trio (Wirth line PL/0 through Oberon; Turbo Pascal as the closed-source demonstration with strict epistemic policy; Brinch Hansen pipeline-of-processes and SuperPascal self-hosting), A192-A193 theory pair (block-structured control flow with WebAssembly single-pass validator; coalgebraic productivity per Rutten, Endrullis, Abel-Pientka, Turner), A194-A196 techniques trio (fixup tables and forward jumps; declare-before-use and forward declarations; symbol tables with scope popping), A197-A198 synthesis pair (integrated single-pass versus decomposed pipeline with Keleusma V0.3.0 as modern worked example; when multi-pass wins with whole-program optimization and Hindley-Milner), A199 closer (the stream processor as compiler and the compiler as stream processor, with full Keleusma compilation pipeline as worked example). Series total 14,273 lines, 90 display equations. Historical claims flagged with epistemic markers throughout, especially A190 Turbo Pascal treatment. Keleusma treatment frames V0.3.0 self-hosting as design-in-progress rather than shipped result. Two-commit publication pattern: draft commit captures all twelve drafts in _drafts/; publication commit batch-moves all twelve to _posts/ with date prefix. Articles back-dated to 2026-04-06 through 2026-04-17, landing flush with the two-dimensional projection series at 2026-04-18. Process files synced (TASKLOG.md, draft_summary.md, REVERSE_PROMPT.md). |

| 2026-07-07 | A200: One-off history-of-hardware-description-languages article researched, drafted, verified against public sources, and published (2026-03-13 back-dated). Three-era organisation covering academic prototypes (ISPS, KARL, ELLA), commercial standardisation era (Verilog, VHDL, SystemVerilog, SystemC, Bluespec), and embedded-DSL revival (Chisel, SpinalHDL, Amaranth, MyHDL, Clash), plus verification track (PSL, SVA, UVM) and high-level synthesis track. Six substantive attribution corrections applied during publication review after WebSearch verification against Wikipedia and project homepages (Verilog Huang co-creator, VHDL Intermetrics/TI/IBM team, SystemC Synopsys origin, Bluespec Hoe co-originator, Amaranth Bourdeauducq/whitequark/2018/2021 timeline, Chisel Par Lab). One display equation formalising Moore's Law as the design-complexity forcing function. Two-commit publication pattern: draft commit then publish-via-git-mv commit plus process file updates. 1903 lines. Categories hdl hardware history. |

| 2026-07-07 | A201: Companion article to A200 researched, drafted, verified against primary sources, and published (2026-07-07). Design-space survey covering four pain points in current industrial HDL flows, four further design levers drawn from adjacent programming-language traditions (static WCET analysis, totality and productivity as type-system properties, coroutine primitives for CDC, static memory footprint analysis), self-hosted synthesis toolchains via Yosys/nextpnr/F4PGA, and cross-domain description languages (SysML v2, Modelica, constructive geometry). Keleusma named directly per user instruction, treated as design-in-progress example implementing software-target analogs of three of the four levers. Kami and Koika cited as MIT formal-verification-integrated HDL research. Von Neumann probe named once as speculative literature. High-assurance embedded control substituted for scrubbed certification-adjacent framing. Zero display equations (design-space survey does not require formalisation beyond what prose supplies). Two-commit publication pattern. 1585 lines. Categories `hdl hardware design`. |
| 2026-07-07 | A201 publication: Article moved from _drafts/ to _posts/2026-07-07-design_space_next_generation_hardware_description_languages.markdown via git mv. Next available article number A202. |
| 2026-07-07 | A202: Companion article to A201 researched, drafted, verified against primary sources, and published (2026-07-08 back-dated by one day for tomorrow's scheduled publication). Historical prior-art survey across four traditions covering the physical-reproduction side of the reproduction loop that A201's self-hosted synthesis toolchains occupy on the computational side. Sections: von Neumann Universal Constructor and the 1966 Burks-edited Theory of Self-Reproducing Automata; the 1980 NASA studies (von Tiesenhausen and Darbro TM-78304 at Marshall in July, and the NASA-ASEE Summer Study at Santa Clara whose 393-page proceedings became CP-2255 edited by Freitas and Gilbreath in November 1982); Freitas and Merkle's 2004 Kinematic Self-Replicating Machines from Landes Bioscience with its 137-dimensional design-space map, funded by Zyvex; RepRap by Adrian Bowyer at University of Bath from 23 March 2005 with first self-print 13 September 2006, Darwin at London Science Museum; industrial digital-twin meta-factories exemplified by Hyundai HMGICS Singapore on NVIDIA Omniverse platform. Closing section synthesises with A201 recording that both computational and mechanical sides of the reproduction loop have established prior art. Two hedges applied during publication review: Freitas-Merkle 137 corrected to reflect design-space dimensionality rather than approach count; NASA seed factory mass softened to order-of-magnitude. Keleusma named briefly with design-in-progress framing and explicit note that meta-factory prior art does not depend on any specific programming language. Von Neumann probe named once with explicit decline to develop the interstellar case. High-assurance embedded control substituted for scrubbed certification-adjacent framing. Zero display equations. Two-commit publication pattern. 1465 lines. Categories `manufacturing self-replication history`. |

| 2026-07-10 | A206-A215 series: Ten-article back-dated historical arc "Developments in Programming Language Theory" researched, drafted, verified against primary sources per article, and published as a single back-dated batch across 2026-03-27 through 2026-04-05. Shared main title "Developments in Programming Language Theory," across all ten articles for reader-facing series identification. A206 opens the arc as a scaffold framing the seventy-year arc from Alonzo Church's lambda calculus through the founding of POPL in October 1973 to present-day production language features, identifying eight recurring threads (type systems, semantics, effect systems, information-flow control, refinement types, dependent types, coroutines and productivity, totality analysis). A207 covers pre-1960 foundations (Church, Turing, Curry-Schönfinkel, Kleene, von Neumann, FORTRAN, LISP, ALGOL 58, ALGOL 60). A208 covers the 1960s (LISP 1.5, Simula and OOP origins, Landin's SECD and ISWIM, McCarthy's mathematical theory, ALGOL 68, structured programming, Hoare's axiomatic method, Scott-Strachey denotational semantics). A209 covers the pragmatic 1970s (structured programming settled, denotational semantics maturation, Dijkstra's discipline, Pascal, C, Prolog, Concurrent Pascal, Modula, Backus's Turing Award critique). A210 covers the theoretical 1970s (POPL founding, Hindley principal types, Milner LCF and first ML, Martin-Löf type theory, Curry-Howard formalization, Denning information-flow lattice). A211 covers the 1980s (Prolog maturation with WAM, Standard ML research program, Miranda and Haskell precursors, category theory as working tool, Lucassen-Gifford effect systems, OOP maturation and OOPSLA founding). A212 covers the 1990s (Standard ML Definition and OCaml, Haskell shipping through Haskell 98, monadic effects reaching practice, Freeman-Pfenning refinement types, Wright-Felleisen type soundness, Coq/PVS/Isabelle/HOL maturation, Myers JFlow, ICFP founding, industrial dynamic languages). A213 covers the 2000s (Pierce Types and Programming Languages consolidation textbook, Coq and Agda maturation with Four Color Theorem and Agda 2, CompCert verified compilation, Rondon-Kawaguchi-Jhala Liquid Types, Siek-Taha gradual typing, HOPL III, new languages Scala/F-sharp/Clojure). A214 covers the 2010s (Rust ownership discipline reaching 1.0 May 2015, F-star and Idris dependent types industrial use, Plotkin-Pretnar effect handlers maturation, Vazou Liquid Haskell as first production refinement-type system, session types industrial use, gradual typing mainstream via TypeScript, Homotopy Type Theory book, new languages Swift/Kotlin/Elm/Elixir/Julia, WebAssembly). A215 covers the 2020s to mid-2026 (HOPL IV virtual 2021, Lean 4 and Mathlib, Coq to Rocq rename 2025, OCaml 5.0 effect handlers December 2022, formal verification pipelines reaching production, refinement types and information-flow labels in embedded scripting including Keleusma, WCET as first-class language property in Keleusma, new languages Zig/Roc/Verse, LLM-assisted programming language work, transition to periodic current-event surveys). Total 14,309 lines across ten articles. All articles mathjax false, zero display equations. Zero em-dashes, en-dashes, contractions, prose semicolons, or certification vocabulary series-wide. | 2026-07-14 | A222: Deep-Concentration Knowledge Work. Standalone one-off article characterizing the class of knowledge workers whose productivity depends on sustained deep concentration. Rolls up one level of abstraction from engineer-specific observation to the class-level property. Three defining cognitive properties identified: extended ramp-up ($T_r$ of order hours), sustained state across days or weeks including sleep, external memory prostheses closing the gap $C_{\text{problem}} \gg M_{\text{working}}$. Cross-profession examples cover software and hardware engineers, mathematicians, long-form writers, composers, experimental scientists, complex-case attorneys, top-level chess and Go players, cryptographers, and architects. Explicit boundary with continuous-shift knowledge work (managers, sales, operations, teaching, customer support). Interruption cost formalized with $L(t_i) = (T_s - t_i) + T_r$ and manager comparison $L_{\text{manager}} = t_{\text{slot}}$. Documentation discipline positioned as consequence of the external memory property. Sections on implications for managers and for practitioners. Nine references: three books (Brooks Mythical Man-Month, Csikszentmihalyi Flow, Newport Deep Work), one essay (Graham Maker's Schedule Manager's Schedule), five primary research papers (Cowan 2001 working memory refinement, Leroy 2009 attention residue, Mark Gudith Klocke 2008 CHI interrupted work, Miller 1956 Magical Number Seven, Rasch Born 2013 sleep and memory). Five cross-references to prior corpus posts (A75 A76 A86 A93 A94). Editorially back-dated to 2026-03-11 09:00 UTC to fill the one-day gap between A105 (2026-03-10) and A103/A106 (2026-03-12). Categories `philosophy management engineering`. 199 lines, mathjax true, three display equations. | 2026-07-14 | A223: Audits and Provenance. Standalone one-off analytical article treating audits at the class level, identifying four properties shared across categories (scope and procedure, independent review, evidence artifacts, findings and remediation), and walking three principal audit categories (engineering audits covering correctness/security/quality, documentation audits covering completeness/currency/accuracy, compliance audits covering regulatory/contractual/internal policy). Positions provenance and audit trails as the substrate infrastructure that determines whether any category is conducted at all rather than as a fourth audit category. Four provenance properties treated: chain of custody, immutability, reconstructibility, retention. Common failure modes catalogued (cargo-cult checklists, adversarial auditee posture, scope creep, retroactive documentation, findings without remediation, single-auditor bias, audit fatigue). Implications for organizations section addresses provenance infrastructure investment, documentation as byproduct of work, internal audit independence structure, audit-frequency calibration, and treatment of findings as work. Three display equations: provenance completeness $p = N_R / N_T$, contemporaneous-versus-retrofit inequality $c N \ll C_R$, audit-coverage bound $\lambda T \leq K$. Five primary references anchor load-bearing claims: DeAngelo 1981 audit-quality theory, Bacchelli and Bird 2013 modern code review empirical study, Anderson 2001 security economics, Buneman Khanna Tan 2001 provenance formalism, Simmhan Plale Gannon 2005 provenance survey. Seven standards references (PCAOB, IIA, NIST SP 800-53, AICPA Trust Services Criteria, ISO 27001, GDPR, W3C PROV) plus two MITRE taxonomies (CWE, CVE). Three cross-references to prior corpus posts (A75 bidirectional agentic workflow, A76 markdown as specification language, A93 fast-moving versus mission-critical engineering). Standalone one-off comparative article treating HTML as one hypermedia model among several historical alternatives (Memex, NLS, Xanadu, HyperCard, BTRON, OpenDoc) and cataloguing the properties that HTML omits or degrades relative to systems that had already established the requirements by the late 1980s. Eight-axis property inventory drawn from the Dexter Hypertext Reference Model: link directionality, link typing, sub-document addressability, transclusion, permanence, versioning, native composition, machine-readable structure. Two display equations: reverse-index staleness bound $N_{\text{stale}} \leq \lambda T$ in the Bidirectional Links section quantifying why external crawls cannot fully recover the bidirectional property, and broken-link decay $f_{\text{broken}}(t) = 1 - 2^{-t / T_{1/2}}$ in the Permanence and Versioning section anchored to Klein 2014 reference-rot measurements. Twenty-two references: four books (Berners-Lee Weaving the Web, Nelson Computer Lib/Dream Machines, Nelson Literary Machines, Nyce and Kahn Memex to Hypertext), six standards and technical reports (Berners-Lee CERN 1989 proposal, Engelbart 1962 conceptual framework, Sakamura TRON, W3C Web Annotation, W3C RDF 1.1, WHATWG HTML), three related posts (A75 bidirectional agentic workflow, A76 markdown as spec, A77 LLM knowledge graphs), and nine primary research papers (Berners-Lee Hendler Lassila 2001 Semantic Web, Bush 1945 As We May Think, Engelbart and English 1968 NLS demo, Halasz 1988 NoteCards, Halasz and Schwartz 1994 Dexter, Klein et al. 2014 reference rot, Meyrowitz 1989 Missing Link, Nelson 1965 File Structure, Nelson 1999 Xanalogical Structure). Opener of a twelve-article rolling-publication series with shared main title "Aerospace, Programming Languages, and Information Technology Co-Development." Establishes the co-development mechanism as a coupled first-order dynamical system with characteristic roots and exponential coupled-growth solutions, characterizes semiconductor manufacturing under defense demand as the physical substrate with the Wright learning-curve formalization of the spillover to commercial markets, formalizes the real-time constraint distinguishing aerospace computing with the flight-control deadline inequality and the Liu-Layland rate-monotonic utilization bound, formalizes the reliability constraint through hardware redundancy and verification-effort scaling, and formalizes the software complexity constraint through the exponential size trajectory and Lehman's laws of software evolution. Introduces the six-axis analytical framework subsequent articles apply: numerical computation demand, real-time control, reliability and verification, networking and distribution, software engineering as discipline, semiconductor economics and dual-use. Preindustrial baseline covers the human-computer bureau under Moulton at Aberdeen, the Bush differential analyzer at MIT, the Ford Instrument Mark 1 fire-control computer for United States Navy capital ships, and Chain Home radar. Series roadmap describes eleven planned subsequent articles at editorial dates 2026-07-13 through 2026-07-23. | 2026-07-18 | A249: Las Vegas: Historical Arc, Present Relevance, and Forward Projection. Standalone one-off case-study article treating Las Vegas across four historical phases (railroad watering stop, mob-era Strip, corporate megaresort transformation, current amenity-priced regime) with point-in-time regional, national, and global relevance assessment as of the mid 2020s and forward projection across 2026 to 2050 under five constraints (water, climate, competition, demographics, pricing power). Core analytical thesis: the historical loss-leader casino model treated non-gaming amenities as subsidies against gambling profit, whereas the contemporary amenity-priced regime treats each amenity as a standalone profit center; the two models optimize different objective functions. Fourteen display equations covering house-edge expected value, aggregate gaming revenue, per-visitor profit, optimal amenity-subsidy condition, non-gaming revenue share, per-visitor revenue decomposition, price elasticity of volume, southern Nevada water balance, per-capita consumption trajectory, water-constrained population ceiling, Macau/Vegas revenue ratio, days-above-105-degrees trajectory, Vegas share of US gaming, and cohort-weighted gambling participation. Forty-two references (eleven books, twenty-eight reference URLs, three research). Primary source anchors include Rothman Neon Metropolis 2002, Schwartz Roll the Bones 2006 and Suburban Xanadu 2003, Reid and Demaris Green Felt Jungle 1963, Reisner Cadillac Desert 1986, Fleck Water is for Fighting Over 2016, Findlay People of Chance 1986, Colorado River Compact 1922, Kefauver Third Interim Report 1951, Nevada Revised Statutes 463 (Corporate Gaming Act), Indian Gaming Regulatory Act 1988, Murphy v. National Collegiate Athletic Association 2018, NOAA Las Vegas climate records, IPCC AR6 WGII Chapter 14, US Census Bureau, UNLV Center for Gaming Research, Nevada Gaming Control Board, Bureau of Reclamation Lake Mead operations, Southern Nevada Water Authority, Macau DICJ, LVCVA, and 10-K filings of MGM Resorts, Wynn Resorts, Las Vegas Sands, Sphere Entertainment, and Liberty Media. Publication review pass corrected water intake elevations (Intake 1 at 1050 ft in 1971, Intake 2 at 1000 ft in 2000, Intake 3 at 860 ft in 2015, Low Lake Level Pumping Station in 2020 enabling withdrawal to 875 ft; Hoover Dam dead pool 895 ft), refined Colorado River Compact history (1922 Compact divided Upper and Lower Basins at 7.5 MAF each, Boulder Canyon Project Act of 1928 apportioned Nevada's 300000 AF share), added epistemic hedges to forward projection paragraph, spelled out RCP and LVS acronyms, updated parking pricing to reflect 2016 launch and 2024 escalation, hedged MGM Empire City licensing claim. Categories `history economics urban`. Editorially back-dated to 2026-01-30 09:00 UTC to fill the one-day gap between A94 constant_amm_mathematics (2026-01-29) and A93 claude_code_getting_started (2026-01-31). 272 lines. Zero em-dashes, en-dashes, contractions, prose colons, prose semicolons, or prose parentheticals outside math notation. Debug tags `<!-- A249 -->` and console.log("A249") on lines 10-11. |

| 2026-08-05 | Corpus-wide pathological word-usage pass (Task 1 of two). Scope 290 target articles and 1,020,372 prose words, excluding the twelve SpaceX articles already remediated for `specific` but including them for `admits`, which that pass did not treat. 110 files changed across four commits. **Measurement.** A first ranking by ratio against the rest of the corpus was discarded as the wrong discriminator, since it surfaces topic vocabulary rather than tics. The working method restricts attention to a fixed list of content-independent words and compares each article against the MEDIAN article rate, the mean being dragged upward by the very articles under investigation. A prose extractor withholds front matter, fenced code, Jekyll `{% highlight %}` blocks, indented code, display math tracked by cumulative delimiter count, headings, tables, link definitions, and reference bullets; inline math, code spans, and Liquid tags are stripped within eligible lines. Counting `{% highlight %}` bodies as prose had put `highlight` and `main` at the top of the first ranking with zero prose uses behind them. **Judgment layer.** A 44-entry exclusion table records why each elevated rate is legitimate, so the filter is auditable: `context` in the context-engineering article, `key` in the Solana and SSH articles, `dimension` in the enhanced-facilities taxonomy, `intrinsic` in intrinsic motivation, `scroll factor` and `sort key` as defined terms, `specific impulse` across the five rocket propellant articles, and `approximately` corpus-wide as quantitative hedging before numerals. **Deletions.** Adjectival `specific` and `specifically` removed where they were filler. The comparative industrialization series used `specifically` as an adjective, which is ungrammatical: 571 of roughly 600 uses were preceded by "the" and followed by a proper adjective, against 2 legitimate adverbial uses in the whole cluster. The aerospace and computing history run of 2026-07-15 through 2026-07-23 carried the same filler `specific` remediated in the SpaceX series but never outside it, peaking at 39.3 per thousand words. Corpus-wide `specific` fell 1715 to 501, from 1.68 to 0.49 per thousand, below the natural rate. **Rotations.** Words that carry meaning were varied rather than deleted, since removal would change claims: `the comprehensive treatments` closed a citation 234 times across the ethnoreligion series; `The framework provides` or `The framework has` opened 106 of 273 sentences in the machines-that-learn series; `X admits the compact form` preceded a display equation 130 times. Rotation is deterministic rather than random so reruns are reproducible and diffs reviewable. `substantial` 1613 to 522, `substantially` 924 to 370, `admits` 755 to 321. **Four bugs caught by verification rather than by reading.** A word-boundary rule spliced hyphenated compounds, turning `case-specific supplementary` into `case-supplementary`. The adverbial guard omitted a trailing word boundary, so its alternation matched prefixes, `in` matching "Indian" and `for` matching "forward", silently protecting roughly 90 adjectival uses. Rotation produced "a extensive" and "the most appreciable", repaired by an article fixer restricted to inserted words and a superlative fixer. Whitespace collapse ran unconditionally and rewrote pre-existing double spaces on 21 lines the word rules never touched. **Also repaired.** Three pre-existing doubled words: "the the service" in two 2016 posts and "various various facilities" in the 2026-01-12 article. **Deliberately not changed.** The residual `framework` rate of 2.08 per thousand is dominated by modified phrases naming distinct technical frameworks, 1379 modified against 149 bare, in a series that surveys learning frameworks. Structural monotony beyond word frequency, such as the repeated sentence pattern "X and Y provide the treatments", was left as an editorial judgment rather than automated. **Pre-existing defects found, not introduced.** An unused `elixir-syslog` anchor in the 2016-01-17 post, and an undefined `rust_book` reference link in the 2025-12-17 Solana post that renders literally as `[The Rust Programming Language][rust_book]` in the built HTML. |
| 2026-08-05 | Documentation and process-file review for pathological instructions (Task 2 of two), with rectification. Scope 31 documents under `_docs/`, plus `CLAUDE.md` and the three shell scripts. **Transmission mechanism of the `specific` pathology, investigated and identified.** The hypothesis that a pathological reference article was being imitated was FALSIFIED by measurement: the two exemplar ranges named in the writing documentation, `A152` through `A160` in `ARTICLE_GENRES.md` and `A153` through `A160` in `SUBSYSTEM_DEEPDIVE_STRUCTURE.md`, measure 0.0 to 1.6 uses of `specific` per thousand words both before and after the remediation pass, against a natural corpus rate near 1.7. The hypothesis of a faulty instruction was also falsified: no writing or process document encourages the word, and none instructs an agent to match sibling prose style. The actual mechanism is a MISSING CHECK. `STYLE_GUIDE.md` constrained punctuation, links, references, and slugs but said nothing about word choice; `PUBLICATION_REVIEW.md` listed its prose-style scope as contractions, dashes, colons, semicolons, parentheticals, and line structure; and the verification script in `STYLE_VERIFICATION.md` counted em-dashes, en-dashes, contractions, math density, and reference integrity. Nothing in the pipeline could observe lexical repetition, so an article at 46.2 uses per thousand passed publication review with prose style reported clean, and the defect survived every context reset because each new session inherited a review process blind to it. **Fixes applied.** A Diction and Repetition section added to `STYLE_GUIDE.md` with four rules and a statement of why the section exists. A Diction and Repetition check added to `PUBLICATION_REVIEW.md`, framed as a flag requiring judgment rather than a verdict, with the three outcomes legitimate, filler, and formulaic. A word-frequency script added to `STYLE_VERIFICATION.md` restricted to content-independent words, since ratio against the corpus surfaces topic vocabulary instead; verified to flag the worst pre-pass article at 39.2 per thousand and to pass the same article after remediation. A catalogue entry added to `COMMON_ERRORS.md` recording the escalation, the falsified hypotheses, and the instruction never to strip a word from an article whose subject is that word. **Configuration contradiction, resolved.** `FORWARD_DATED_POSTS.md` asserted `future: true` in five places, quoted a fabricated `_config.yml` excerpt asserting it, called the setting foundational, and stated that a post may cross-reference a forward-dated post in either direction. The live configuration is `future: false`. The cross-reference claim was not merely stale but actively harmful, and rather than reason about it a minimal two-post site was built under `future: false`, which failed with `Could not find post "2027-01-01-futurepost" in tag 'post_url'` and `ERROR: YOUR SITE COULD NOT BE BUILT`. The document was rewritten against verified behaviour and carries a dated correction notice. The same error had propagated to `GLOSSARY.md` twice, `CONTENT_WORKFLOW.md` twice, `CROSS_LINKED_SERIES.md`, and `process/README.md`, all corrected. `CROSS_LINKED_SERIES.md` additionally claimed that staging a batch together makes all internal tags resolve, which holds only when every article in the batch is back-dated; the three safe date configurations are now enumerated. **Broken tooling, repaired.** `_publish.sh`, the documented publication mechanism referenced from 25 documentation locations, extracted the date with the bracket expression `[+-:0-9 ]`, which BSD sed rejects as an invalid character range, so on macOS it reported every file as undated and silently moved nothing while exiting zero. The hyphen was moved to the front, which is portable across BSD and GNU sed, and extraction was confirmed to return a date where it previously returned none. **Other corrections.** `CROSS_LINKED_SERIES.md` had directed reliance on the deploy build as authoritative verification because the local bundle is broken, which amounts to publishing unverified and letting the live site find the errors; it now carries the Gemfile-free scratch-copy build procedure. `STYLE_VERIFICATION.md` wrote scratch to the system `/tmp` against the project-local `tmp/` convention, and stated "expect 200 responses across the board" without recording that a 200 does not verify a citation, the failure mode behind thirteen fabricated citations found on 2026-08-02. `CLAUDE.md` now records that `_preview.sh` passes `--future` while the live site sets `future: false`, so a clean preview is not evidence that a forward-dated cross-reference is safe. A relative-link example in `DOCUMENTATION_STRATEGY.md` was corrected to show resolution from the linking file's own directory. Zero broken relative links across all documentation after the edits. |
| 2026-08-05 | Infrastructure audit and remediation. **PDF downloads were failing for every post while CI reported success.** Each post page links `<permalink>.pdf`; a sample of twelve returned 404 for all twelve, and the CI log's own failure summary listed every post tagged `(pdf)`. Root cause was `! LaTeX Error: File 'lmodern.sty' not found`, since the workflow installed `texlive-xetex`, `texlive-fonts-recommended`, `texlive-latex-recommended`, `texlive-latex-extra`, `fonts-dejavu`, and `fonts-noto-cjk` but not `lmodern`, which pandoc's default LaTeX template loads. The step still reported success because `_downloads.rb` only warned, so roughly 293 dead download links shipped undetected across many deploys. Fixed by adding `lmodern` to the apt install list, and by making `_downloads.rb` exit nonzero on SYSTEMIC failure, defined as a format producing zero outputs across all posts, which indicates a broken toolchain rather than bad content. Individual failures still only warn so one malformed post cannot block a deploy. **EPUB files were written to paths with no matching HTML.** `_downloads.rb` reconstructed each permalink from the source FILENAME date while Jekyll derives it from the front matter `date:` converted to the build machine timezone. Nineteen posts resolve to a different day than their filename implies, sixteen of them 2016 posts stamped `+0900` in the early morning, so their EPUB landed in a directory with no HTML, producing a 404 link on the post page and an orphan file in the artifact. Fixed by discovering the emitted HTML with a glob over the built site rather than computing the path, which sidesteps date arithmetic and timezone configuration entirely. Verified against a real build: 293 of 294 posts resolve to exactly one HTML file and would receive correctly placed downloads, the single exception being the forward-dated 2026-08-04 article that is legitimately absent from the build. **Permalinks depended on the build machine's timezone.** `_config.yml` set no `timezone:`, so Jekyll fell back to the system zone. The post stamped `2026-03-06 01:14:26 +0000` builds as `/2026/03/06/` on the UTC runner, which is the live URL, but as `/2026/03/05/` on a machine in Pacific time, meaning a local URL check was not evidence about production. Pinned `timezone: UTC`, which is what the runner already uses so no existing URL changes. Verified by comparing a local build against the live sitemap: 291 local post pages and 291 live post pages with zero differences in either direction. **Reference integrity restored corpus-wide.** Added the missing `[rust_book]` definition to the 2025-12-17 Solana article, where `[The Rust Programming Language][rust_book]` had been rendering literally on the live site, and removed four orphan definitions in 2016 articles. Corpus now reports zero undefined anchors and zero unused definitions. **Dependency.** Bumped `json` from 2.19.7 to 2.21.2 in `Gemfile.lock`, clearing the open low-severity Dependabot advisory whose first patched version was 2.19.9; the lockfile diff is that single line. **Documentation.** Recorded in `CROSS_LINKED_SERIES.md` that the Gemfile-free scratch build strips `jekyll-archives` and does not run `_downloads.rb`, so category, tag, and download links appear broken in it. A link crawl of that build reported 740 broken targets on that basis alone, every one of them fine in production. Also recorded that permalinks in that build now match production because of the timezone pin. **Audited and found healthy:** all top-level pages, feeds, sitemap, robots, favicon, manifest, and the Pagefind index return 200; zero broken internal links and zero missing asset references; no duplicate article numbers, no front matter defects, and complete debug-tag coverage across 294 posts; MathJax flags correct, with all six apparent unrendered-math cases proving to be shell variables, Makefile syntax, or a currency figure; category and tag archives live, including `/categories/keleusma/` which does not hit the shadowed-path trap; `draft_summary.md` in sync with all eight drafts; next article number A295 consistent with the highest present A294; no build artifacts tracked; the four unresolved draft `post_url` tags are the documented and expected interlock condition. |
| 2026-08-05 | PDF download repair, second iteration. The first fix was deployed and FAILED, which is the intended behaviour of the systemic-failure guard added alongside it. Adding `lmodern` cleared the original error, and the run then surfaced the next missing dependency, `! LaTeX Error: File 'xeCJK.sty' not found`, with `pdf=0 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=293`. The guard exited nonzero, the workflow went red, and GitHub Pages continued serving the previous successful deploy, so the live site never regressed. Under the previous warn-only behaviour this second failure would have shipped silently exactly as the first one did. Two changes address it. First, `texlive-lang-chinese` is added to the apt list, since it supplies `xeCJK.sty`. Second, and more importantly, `_downloads.rb` now passes `CJKmainfont` only for documents that actually contain CJK characters. Passing it unconditionally made pandoc's template load `xeCJK.sty` for every post, so all 294 PDFs depended on a package that only 2 posts need, those being the 2016-10-01 Lumina article at 88 CJK characters and the 2016-02-23 Unicode counting article at 20. Scoping the option means a gap in the CJK toolchain can no longer fail the other 292, and any residual CJK failure is an individual failure that only warns rather than a systemic one that blocks the deploy. The detection regex was unit-tested against Japanese, Chinese, Korean, mixed, and ASCII-only inputs, and confirmed to select exactly 2 of 294 posts. The EPUB path correction from the previous iteration is CONFIRMED working by the same failed run: `epub=293 skipped=1`, meaning every built post produced an EPUB beside its HTML and the single skip is the forward-dated 2026-08-04 article legitimately absent from the build, against a prior state where nineteen EPUBs were written to directories containing no HTML. **Method note.** The deploy was watched with `gh run watch --exit-status` chained ahead of another command, so the shell reported exit 0 from the trailing command while the run itself concluded `failure`. The conclusion was read directly from `gh run view` rather than inferred from the chained exit status. Do not infer a CI verdict from a chained shell exit code. |
| 2026-08-05 | PDF download repair, third iteration, and the deploy that confirmed it. The second iteration DEPLOYED SUCCESSFULLY with `pdf=256 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=37`, verified live: PDF, EPUB, and HTML all return 200 for sampled posts, including the relocated 2026-03-06 article whose downloads previously landed in a directory with no HTML. The `[rust_book]` repair is also confirmed live, rendering as a link to `doc.rust-lang.org/book` with no literal `][rust_book]` remaining. Two further defects were then diagnosed from that run's log. **Code was being typographed as prose.** `preprocess` stripped `{% highlight %}` and `{% endhighlight %}` markers but left the code between them as ordinary paragraph text, so pandoc applied smart quotes and `printf("Hello, World!")` reached LaTeX as `printf(“Hello, World!` with curly quotes, alongside unescaped braces, backslashes, and dollar signs. That produced `! Undefined control sequence` and killed the PDF for 36 posts, every one of them from the era when this blog used Liquid highlight tags rather than backtick fences. Fixed by converting highlight blocks into fenced code blocks BEFORE the generic Liquid strip, since the strip would otherwise remove the delimiters and expose the code. `{% raw %}` blocks are now unwrapped rather than emptied. Verified without pandoc by running the real `preprocess` over three failing posts and asserting balanced fence markers, zero leftover Liquid, and zero code lines outside a fence. **Images were silently absent from every download.** Markdown carries site-absolute references as `/assets/...`, which is correct for the web but reads as a filesystem absolute path to pandoc, which reported `withBinaryFile: does not exist` and emitted the document without the image. Fixed by rewriting those references to the built site on disk. Verified against a real build: 11 rewritten references, 0 missing. **Residual.** A small number of legacy 2016 posts additionally carry malformed math that pandoc rejects, including an amsmath nesting error and a bad math environment delimiter, most likely in the 2016-09-02 sphere-equation and 2016-09-03 trigonometry articles. These are individual failures that only warn, so they cannot block a deploy, and they were not chased further because diagnosing LaTeX without a local pandoc is guesswork. |
| 2026-08-05 | PDF download repair complete, and two genuine math defects found in published articles. The third iteration DEPLOYED SUCCESSFULLY with `pdf=290 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=3`, down from 293 failures. Live verification confirms the previously failing Liquid-highlight-era posts now serve PDFs at 200, including the 2016 single-file Elixir, UNIX ARM assembler, and 2022 no_std Rust articles. The three residual failures were then diagnosed and were not toolchain problems at all but invalid LaTeX that MathJax had been rendering leniently. **Align wrapped in display delimiters.** The 2016-09-02 sphere-equation and 2016-09-03 trigonometry articles wrapped `\begin{align}` inside `$$ ... $$`, 14 and 20 blocks respectively. The `align` environment is already display math, so nesting it produces `! Package amsmath Error: Erroneous nesting of equation structures`. Every occurrence in both files was verified to be wrapped, 34 of 34, then converted to `aligned`, which is the environment intended for use inside math mode and is valid for both MathJax and LaTeX. Delimiter counts confirmed balanced afterward. **A double subscript in a published SpaceX article.** Line 454 of the 2026-07-28 decomposability article read `I_{sp}^{\text{Merlin 1D}}_{\text{sea-level}}`, which attaches a second subscript to a base that already carries one and is invalid LaTeX, producing `! Double subscript`. Rewritten as `I_{sp}^{\text{Merlin 1D, sea level}}`, matching the style of the sibling term `I_{sp}^{\text{Merlin 1D Vacuum}}` in the same equation. A corpus-wide scan for the same pattern found no other instances. **Method note.** These defects were invisible to every existing check. The style verification script counts delimiters and reports balance, which was correct in both cases, and MathJax renders leniently enough that the pages looked fine. The PDF pipeline is the first process in this project that parses the math strictly, so it functions as a math linter for the corpus. That is a reason to keep PDF generation healthy beyond the downloads themselves. |
| 2026-08-05 | PDF failures reduced to three, then those three diagnosed as further invalid LaTeX rather than tooling. The fourth deploy succeeded and held at `pdf=290 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=3`, but the ERRORS CHANGED, which confirmed the previous round of math repairs landed and exposed the next layer. Three distinct causes, all of them constructs MathJax renders leniently and LaTeX rejects. **A second double subscript that an earlier scan missed.** Line 472 of the 2026-07-28 decomposability article carried `I_{sp}^{\text{Raptor 2}}_{\text{sea-level}}`, the same defect as the Merlin term repaired earlier. The first corpus scan reported zero remaining because its pattern used `[^}]*` for the superscript, which cannot span `\text{...}`, so every double subscript whose superscript contained braces was invisible to it. Replaced with a brace-matching scan, which found the Raptor term and, after repair, reports zero across posts and drafts. The lesson is that a regex over LaTeX must respect brace nesting or it silently under-reports. **Array column specifications narrower than their rows.** The 2016-09-03 trigonometry article declared `\begin{array}{c|lcr}`, four columns, for tables whose rows carry seven and six columns, raising `! Extra alignment tab has been changed to \cr`. Widths were recomputed from the widest row in each block and the specifications corrected to `{c|cccccc}` and `{c|ccccc}`. A third array in the same file was left alone because its four declared columns already exceed its three-column rows. **A MathJax-only macro.** The 2016-09-02 sphere-equation article uses `\bbox[cyan]{...}` fifteen times for emphasis. It is a MathJax extension with no LaTeX equivalent, so xelatex aborted with an undefined control sequence. Stripping it from the source would have silently changed the appearance of a published page, so instead `_downloads.rb` now passes `header-includes=\providecommand{\bbox}[2][]{#2}` to pandoc, which teaches LaTeX to discard the colour argument and typeset the content. `\providecommand` is used rather than `\newcommand` so a real definition would not be clobbered. The HTML rendering is untouched. |
| 2026-08-05 | PDF failures reduced to two, then those two diagnosed. The fifth deploy succeeded at `pdf=291 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=2`; the Raptor 2 double subscript repair landed and the 2026-07-28 article now generates. The remaining two are both 2016 math articles with two further causes. **MathJax spellings of comparison operators.** `\lt` and `\gt` have no LaTeX definition. They surfaced only after the `\bbox` shim landed, because LaTeX reports one undefined control sequence per run and `\bbox` had been masking them. Added `\providecommand` shims for both alongside the existing `\bbox` shim, so the source keeps its MathJax spelling and the HTML is untouched. Note that `\le` in the same article is valid LaTeX and needs no shim. **An opening math delimiter followed by a space.** Line 131 of the trigonometry article read `the form $ \operatorname{f} ...`, and pandoc's `tex_math_dollars` requires that the opening delimiter not be followed by whitespace, so it treated the `$` as a literal dollar sign and `\operatorname` landed in text mode, raising `! Missing $ inserted`. MathJax is more permissive and had always rendered it, so the page looked correct. Removed the space. A corpus-wide scan for an opening `$` followed by whitespace and then a LaTeX command found this as the only occurrence across all posts and drafts. **Pattern worth recording.** Each round of this repair has revealed the next defect rather than exhausting the list, because LaTeX aborts at the first error in a document. Counting failures per deploy is therefore a poor progress signal; the informative check is whether the ERROR TEXT changed, which is what confirmed each previous fix had landed. |
| 2026-08-05 | Download pipeline fully repaired and verified. The sixth deploy reports `pdf=293 epub=293 skipped=1 failed=0`, from an initial state of 293 PDF failures and 19 misplaced EPUBs. Live verification over a 28-post sample spanning 2016 to 2026, including the two articles repaired in the final round, returns 200 for BOTH formats on every one. The single skip is the forward-dated 2026-08-04 article, correctly absent from the build until its date arrives. Six deploys were required because LaTeX aborts at the first error in a document, so each repair exposed the next defect rather than exhausting the list. Root causes in order: a missing `lmodern` package; a missing `texlive-lang-chinese` package, subsequently made non-fatal by scoping the CJK font request to the 2 posts that need it; Liquid highlight blocks left as prose so pandoc typographed code into smart quotes, which accounted for 36 failures; site-absolute asset paths pandoc could not resolve, so images were silently absent from every download; `align` nested inside display delimiters in 2 articles, 34 blocks; two double subscripts; two under-declared array column specifications; the MathJax-only macros `\bbox`, `\lt`, and `\gt`; and one opening math delimiter followed by a space. Of these, six were latent defects IN THE CORPUS rather than tooling gaps, all of them invalid LaTeX that MathJax renders leniently and that the existing delimiter-balance check could not detect. **Standing conclusion.** The PDF pipeline is the only process in this project that parses the mathematics strictly, so it functions as a math linter for the corpus. Keeping it green has value beyond the downloads themselves, and `_downloads.rb` now exits nonzero on systemic failure so a regression cannot ship silently as the original defect did. |
